# Roadmap — Chickadee / iptv_apps
**Maturity:** HIGH (~95% — live, multi-platform). **Stopping point:** once DVR+VOD+multistream ship and it's in both app stores, it's feature-complete → maintenance + content-ops only. Don't chase Fubo's channel count.

## P1 — competitive parity + store launch (next ~2 quarters)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Cloud DVR — record/list/delete, retention policy, RecordingsView — every rival has it {cat: backend+web; size: L; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] VOD / on-demand catalog + tab — Sling Freestream angle {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Multiple simultaneous streams (plan-based limit + 409 guard) {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] App Store + Play Store submission blockers — RevenueCat is already SDK-wired for iOS/Android with API keys and product IDs configured, but Apple Guideline 3.1.1 and Google Play's subscription policy both mandate native in-app purchase (not Stripe) for any digital subscription bought inside the app, so remaining IAP work is finishing App Store Connect/Play Console product setup plus sandbox testing, not new integration. The bigger blocker is content rights: Chickadee's Xtream/M3U playlist-import architecture is exactly the pattern Apple's 5.2.2/5.2.3 guidelines target, and reviewers will require documentary proof — a signed licensing contract or authorization letter from each channel/content rights-holder — attached in App Review Information, or rejection is near-certain. That proof-of-rights posture is a business call Shrike Labs must make (license specific channels vs. reposition as a bring-your-own-already-licensed-playlist player with matching ToS/UI), and can't be resolved by coding alone. Sequencing: finish RevenueCat/IAP store-side config and sandbox tests now (buildable), settle the content-rights/licensing decision next, then submit with the authorization documentation attached last. {cat: infra; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines (3.1.1/5.2.2/5.2.3), support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10281818}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] YouTube support follow-through — an MVP already exists (curated news/government/space/local channels as a free-content Discover source, Sling-Freestream-style differentiation: backend CuratedYouTubeChannel model + youtube_channel_checker.py quota-aware Data API v3 poller + youtube_channel_job.py APScheduler 30min job + GET /api/discover/youtube, tested via test_youtube_channel_checker.py + test_discover_youtube_endpoint.py; web DiscoverView.vue YouTube tab; iOS YouTubeChannelPlayerView.swift; Android YoutubePlayerActivity.kt) — this item is the remaining gaps: (1) `GET /api/discover/youtube` has NO auth/premium-tier gate — every other stream source goes through app/services/premium.py's is_premium/get_stream_limit, this one bypasses it entirely, decide + implement the gate; (2) it's architecturally siloed from the core Stream model (app/models/__init__.py) used by every other source — decide whether that's fine long-term (curated-only, never mixed with user-added M3U/Xtream sources) or whether it should unify; (3) Android has an UNCOMMITTED local fix for the WebView black-screen bug (two confirmed root causes via live DevTools instrumentation: the embedded WebView's " wv" User-Agent token makes YouTube serve a degraded/blackened player, AND a CSS 0-height layout race from percentage-height chaining before the WebView is measured — fixed by stripping " wv" from the UA and switching to position:fixed + viewport units) sitting in YoutubePlayerActivity.kt — needs a build/lint verification pass (no Android build tooling available in this session to verify), removal of the leftover `WebView.setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled(true)` temp-debug line, and this repo has ZERO Android instrumented tests for this file — add some; (4) no admin/editorial flow exists to add/curate channels — only via hand-written Alembic migrations; (5) no tvOS YouTube playback found; (6) add E2E coverage (Playwright/Appium/XCUITest) for the Discover YouTube tab — none exists; (7) silent-warning-only startup check for a missing YOUTUBE_API_KEY should probably hard-fail or alert instead. {cat: backend+web+mobile; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
## P2 — retention + polish (~q3-4)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Reliable playback: rebuffer backoff, error recovery, cast/AirPlay hardening {cat: web+mobile; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Easy self-serve cancellation + billing transparency (beat competitor gripes) {cat: web+backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Watchlist/Continue-watching parity across web + mobile {cat: web+mobile; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
## P3 — differentiation (~year 2)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Profiles + parental controls depth; kids mode — Chickadee already has `Profile`/`ParentalSettings`/rating-hierarchy models, enforcement services, and a `ProfilesView.vue` UI, so scope this as finishing PIN-gated profile switching end-to-end and wiring `kid_max_rating`/`blocked_categories` into stream+discover queries on web/iOS/Android, plus a simplified kids-mode UI (bigger tiles, filtered grid) rather than a separate app — mirroring Plex's preset restriction-profile+PIN-unlock model and Sling's 2026 Minor's Profile (grid-level rating filter, no bespoke content-review team). Avoid building a custom rating taxonomy or per-title manual review queue — reuse the existing TV-Y…TV-MA/G…NC-17 hierarchy already in `parental.py`. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Plex Support - Parental Controls (support.plex.tv), Sling TV parental controls guide (thestreamable.com)}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Personalized discover/recommendations — Chickadee's `StreamDiscoveryService` already exposes `/personalized`, `/similar`, `/genre`, `/trending` endpoints and a `ForYouRow.vue` component, so scope this as tuning/finishing that existing heuristic engine (genre-tag matching via `Content.genres`/`Stream.category`, recency/region-based trending, continue-watching-adjacent suggestions) and fully surfacing it in `DiscoverView.vue`, not building a new ML pipeline. This matches how small catalogs get value from content-based/metadata filtering plus light curation (e.g. HBO Max's human-curated + algorithm hybrid) rather than collaborative-filtering ML, which would be disproportionate infra/maintenance for Chickadee's catalog size — explicitly avoid adding a model-training pipeline. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Content-based Recommendation Engine for Video Streaming (arxiv.org/pdf/2308.08406), AI-Based Personalisation in Streaming Platforms (miquido.com)}

## Maintenance — small bug fixes & coverage gaps found in code (2026-09-17, roadmap was dry, refueled)

Android's `EpgRepository.kt` (iptv-android/app/src/main/java/com/chickadeestreams/iptv/data/repository/EpgRepository.kt) has `createSource()`/`refreshSource()`/`createMapping()` (one-channel-at-a-time manual mapping) but no `autoMap()` method; iOS's `APIService+Extended.swift` (iptv-ios/ChickadeeStreams/Services/APIService+Extended.swift) has `addEPGSource()`/`refreshEPGSource()` with the identical gap. Scope: add an `autoMap(sourceId)` call to both `EpgRepository.kt` and `APIService+Extended.swift` hitting the existing endpoint, and invoke it automatically right after a successful `createSource()`/`addEPGSource()` (mirroring iptv-web's GuideView.vue call site) so users don't need a separate manual step. - [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Android targetSdk is still 34; Google Play requires 36 for new-app submissions as of a deadline that has already passed — `iptv-android/app/build.gradle.kts` sets `compileSdk = 34` and `targetSdk = 34` (confirmed via `grep -n 'targetSdk\|compileSdk' app/build.gradle.kts`), but Google Play policy (per SUBMISSION_CHECKLIST.md's 2026-09-16 "🔴 NEW" note, confirmed against developer.android.com) requires **targetSdk 36 (Android 16)** for new app submissions since **August 31, 2026** — already past, and this app has not yet been submitted. ## Stopping point
When P1+P2 ship and stores accept it: FEATURE-COMPLETE. After that, only content/EPG ops, bug-fixes, and OS-version upkeep. Do NOT add live-TV-provider-scale features.

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] The search endpoint completely bypasses the per-kid-profile parental rating ceiling that every other browse surface enforces — `iptv-backend/app/routers/search.py`'s `search_streams()` (line 77) and its `_base_visible_query()` helper (line 65) only exclude the 9-keyword `_ADULT_KEYWORDS` list when `include_adult=False`, and never reference `active_profile`/`get_active_profile`/`profile_rating_ceiling` at all — confirmed via `grep -n 'profile_rating_ceiling\|MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS\|active_profile\|get_active_profile' iptv-backend/app/routers/search.py` returning nothing, and `search_streams()`'s own signature has no `Depends(get_active_profile)` parameter (unlike every other data-browsing router in the app: `grep -rln 'Depends(get_active_profile)' iptv-backend/app/routers` lists streams.py, downloads.py, reminders.py, discover.py, favorites.py, watch_history.py, parental.py, notifications.py — search.py is the one outlier). `streams.py`'s main stream-listing endpoint (the primary browse/grid view), by contrast, applies BOTH the general adult-keyword filter AND a second `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` filter gated on `profile_rating_ceiling(db, current_user.id, active_profile)` (lines 216-225) specifically to enforce a kid profile's rating ceiling regardless of the `include_adult` flag. Net effect: a kid profile that is correctly blocked from browsing "TV-MA"/mature-rated categories in the normal grid view can trivially find and open (or favorite/download) the exact same content by typing it into search instead, since search.py never checks which profile is active. Add the same `active_profile: Optional[Profile] = Depends(get_active_profile)` + `profile_rating_ceiling()`-gated `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` filter to `search.py`'s `_base_visible_query()`/`search_streams()` that `streams.py` already applies. VERIFY: new pytest asserting a kid-ceiling profile's `GET /search?q=...` never returns a stream whose category matches `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS`, matching the existing ceiling test pattern for the streams-listing endpoint. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Three independently-maintained adult/mature keyword lists have drifted apart, so the general "hide adult content" toggle (used by every account, not just kid profiles) is less strict than the kid-profile ceiling check — `iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py`'s `_ADULT_KEYWORDS` (line 90) and `iptv-backend/app/routers/search.py`'s own separately-defined `_ADULT_KEYWORDS` (line 34) are identical 9-term lists (`XXX, PORN, ADULT, EROTIC, HARDCORE, X-RATED, XRATED, 18+, HENTAI`), but `iptv-backend/app/services/parental_controls.py`'s `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` (line 32) is a superset with three extra terms: `MATURE`, `TV-MA`, `NC-17`. `_ADULT_KEYWORDS` is what backs the plain `include_adult=False` query flag (`streams.py` line 216, `search.py` line 70) — used any time ANY account (not just a rating-ceiling'd kid profile) wants adult content hidden — so a stream categorized only as e.g. "TV-MA Drama" or "Mature Content" would NOT be hidden by that toggle even though it's exactly the kind of content `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` was built to catch for kid profiles. Consolidate to one shared keyword list (import `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` from `parental_controls.py` into both routers, or extract a shared constant module) instead of three independently-edited copies, matching the "duplicate keyword/threshold lists drift over time" lesson already learned elsewhere in this codebase (favorites_ordering/cleanDisplayTitle partial-landing items on this same roadmap). {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}

## Newly discovered (2026-09-21 refuel — backlog low (5) and 0 [ready] items; Claude research pass)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] iOS and Android's "Favorites" screens still write to the legacy per-STREAM `is_favorite` flag, not the per-PROFILE `UserFavorite` API — so a household's kid profile and parent profile silently share one favorites list on mobile, unlike web — `iptv-backend/app/models/favorites.py`'s `UserFavorite` is uniquely keyed on `(user_id, profile_id, stream_id)` specifically so different profiles under one account (Chickadee's real, shipped parental-controls/Profile feature) can each have their own favorites, and `app/routers/favorites.py`'s `GET/POST/DELETE /api/favorites` all correctly scope by `active_profile_id(active_profile)` (confirmed via grep: `Depends(get_active_profile)` used throughout). `iptv-web/src/stores/favorites.ts`'s `addFavorite()`/`toggleFavorite()` correctly call this real per-profile API. But Android's `FavoritesScreen.kt` → `StreamsViewModel.toggleFavorite()` → `StreamRepository.toggleFavorite()` and iOS's `StreamsListView.swift`/`StreamsViewModel.swift` → `APIService.toggleFavorite(streamId:isFavorite:)` both instead PUT/PATCH the legacy `Stream.is_favorite` boolean (confirmed via grep across both codebases — zero references to `/api/favorites` anywhere in `iptv-android/app/src/main` or `iptv-ios/ChickadeeStreams`, only `is_favorite`/`isFavorite` query-param and body usage). `Stream.is_favorite` lives on the `Stream` row itself, which is NOT profile-scoped — so on mobile, whichever profile last toggled a channel's favorite state changes it for every other profile on the same account, and a kid profile's favorites list is not actually isolated from what a parent favorited (or vice versa) despite the app's parental-controls feature implying that separation. (Distinct from Watchlist, which is already correctly wired to the real per-profile `UserWatchlist` API on both mobile platforms — this bug is specific to Favorites.) Scope: add `getFavorites()`/`addFavorite()`/`removeFavorite()` methods to a new `FavoritesRepository.kt` (mirroring the existing Watchlist repository pattern) hitting `/api/favorites`, and equivalent methods to iOS's `APIService+Extended.swift` (mirroring its existing Watchlist methods), then repoint `FavoritesScreen`'s star-toggle and `StreamsListView(initialFavoritesOnly:)`'s filtering at the real per-profile API instead of `is_favorite`. VERIFY: new repository/viewmodel test on each platform asserting toggling a favorite as one profile doesn't appear for a second profile on the same account. {cat: android+ios; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

Scope: change `content_id: UUID` to `content_id: int` in `get_content_performance()`'s signature (matching the sibling `get_viewing_patterns`/`get_trending_content` endpoints in the same file, which take no ID param, and matching every other real Stream-ID path param in this codebase, e.g. `discovery_api.py`'s `/similar/{channel_id}: int`). The backing service method is currently a stub returning `{"implemented": False, ...}` and never queries the DB with `channel_id`, so this is not yet an active production bug, but it means the endpoint cannot even be exercised with realistic input once someone implements the real logic, and no test (`grep -rn "channels/add\|add_channel_to_recommendations" iptv-backend/tests/*.py` — confirm coverage before changing) currently calls it through the real HTTP layer either. Scope: change `AddChannelRequest.channel_id: UUID` to `channel_id: int` and `add_channel_to_recommendations`'s `channel_id: UUID` parameter to `channel_id: int`, matching the sibling `/similar/{channel_id}` convention. VERIFY: `grep -n "channel_id: UUID" iptv-backend/app/routers/discovery_api.py iptv-backend/app/services/stream_discovery_service.py` returns nothing after the fix; add a router-level test posting `{"channel_id": 1, "genres": ["news"]}` to `/api/discovery/channels/add` and asserting no 422. The real, actually-wired router lives in the similarly-named `app/routers/admin_youtube.py` (prefix `/api/admin/youtube`, sync `Session`, full CRUD, proper `CuratedYouTubeChannelAdminOut` response schema, lists both active AND inactive channels) — the orphan's own docstring-less single GET-only endpoint uses an `AsyncSession` inconsistently with the rest of this codebase's routers (documented convention: sync `Session` everywhere except a few `AsyncSession`-based routers like `discovery_api.py`), returns raw ORM objects with no response_model (a field-leak risk if it were ever wired), and only shows active channels. ## Refuel 2026-09-25 (Claude research pass — roadmap was fully [decomposed]/dry)

Direct-code-verified against the live `develop` checkout (not just the roadmap snapshot) — every "confirmed via grep" claim below was re-run against the actual repo today, 2026-09-25. Given how heavily this backlog has already been mined for dead-code/duplication bugs across six prior refuel rounds, this pass looked instead for whole-feature gaps: things nobody has ever built, not lines nobody wired up. Weighted toward retention/monetization/compliance since those hit paying subscribers directly, plus one platform-parity item that's a genuine App Store submission risk.

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Self-service password-reset flow — `iptv-backend/app/routers/auth.py` exposes exactly five endpoints (`register`, `login`, `refresh`, `me`, `logout`) and zero reset/forgot-password endpoints (confirmed via direct read of the full file); `iptv-web/src/views/LoginView.vue` has no "forgot password" link (confirmed via grep for `forgot`/`reset` — zero hits). A paying subscriber who forgets their password today has no recovery path except emailing support directly. `iptv-backend/app/services/emailer.py`'s SendGrid helper (already live, used for feedback mail) makes this cheap to build: add a reset-token table, `POST /api/auth/password/forgot` + `POST /api/auth/password/reset` to `auth.py`, and `ForgotPasswordView.vue`/`ResetPasswordView.vue` under `iptv-web/src/views/` linked from `LoginView.vue`. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Account data export + self-service account deletion — confirmed via repo-wide grep that zero `export_data`/`delete_account`/`gdpr` functionality exists anywhere in `iptv-backend/app`, and `iptv-web/src/views/SettingsView.vue` has no delete-account control at all. Apple App Review Guideline 5.1.1(v) requires any app that lets a user create an account to also let them delete it in-app — the same guideline family (5.x) that already blocked this app on the missing consent checkbox (fixed 2026-08-19 per ROADMAP.md), so this isn't optional pre-launch polish, it's a plausible rejection reason the moment this gets resubmitted. Add `GET /api/account/export` (a JSON dump of the caller's own `Stream`/`WatchHistory`/`UserFavorite`/`UserWatchlist`/`Subscription`/`Profile` rows) and `DELETE /api/account` (cancels any active Stripe/RevenueCat subscription first, then cascade-deletes) to a new `app/routers/account.py`, plus a "Download my data" / "Delete my account" section with a confirmation dialog in `SettingsView.vue`. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] iOS Privacy Manifest (`PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`) is missing entirely — a real, current App Store submission blocker — confirmed via `find iptv-ios -iname '*.xcprivacy'` (excluding the `build/` tree) returning zero results anywhere in the app's own source. Apple has required a top-level `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` (declaring required-reason API usage and data-collection categories) since Spring 2024 for any app whose dependency tree touches "required reason" APIs (UserDefaults, file-timestamp APIs, etc.) — and this app bundles RevenueCat, Alamofire, and Kingfisher (all confirmed present under `iptv-ios/build/SourcePackages/checkouts/`), each of which touches at least one such API. Missing manifests are a documented, active App Store Connect rejection/warning reason, not a theoretical one. Add a `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` to the `ChickadeeStreams` target declaring this app's own data collection (account email, watch history, crash/analytics if any), and verify the vendored SDKs' own manifests are being picked up by Xcode's manifest aggregation. {cat: ios; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Failed-payment dunning + subscription win-back emails — confirmed via grep that zero `dunning`/`win_back`/`churn` references exist anywhere in `iptv-backend/app`, despite `app/services/revenuecat.py`'s webhook already receiving billing-issue/expiration events and `app/models/subscription.py`'s `SubscriptionStatus` already distinguishing active/canceled/expired states. No email of any kind fires today when a renewal fails or a subscription lapses — `app/services/emailer.py` (SendGrid, already wired for feedback mail) sits unused for this. Add a dunning email fired from the webhook handler on a billing-issue event, and a separate win-back email sent by a new APScheduler job (mirroring `app/jobs/reminders_job.py`'s existing pattern) N days after a subscription lapses to `CANCELED`/`EXPIRED`, with a one-tap resubscribe link. FEATURE_ROADMAP.md already named "dunning copy for failed renewals" as a Phase-4 goal (item 13) but it was never built — this decomposes that gap into real scope. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] In-app store-rating prompt — confirmed via grep that no `SKStoreReviewController`/`requestReview` exists anywhere in `iptv-ios/ChickadeeStreams` (the only hits are false positives inside the vendored `iptv-ios/build/` package checkouts) and no Play In-App Review API (`ReviewManager`/`ReviewManagerFactory`) exists anywhere in `iptv-android/app/src/main`. This app has shipped on both stores with zero organic rating-prompt mechanism — a near-zero-cost, well-established lever for store ranking that's easy to underinvest in on a solo-founder app. Add `SKStoreReviewController.requestReview(in:)` on iOS (fire after a sensible positive moment, e.g. N completed playback sessions, tracked via a simple counter alongside `iptv-ios/ChickadeeStreams/Services/KeychainService.swift`'s storage) and the Play Core `ReviewManager` flow on Android, each capped by the OS's own once-per-cooldown policy so no custom rate-limiting is needed. {cat: android+ios; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Email verification on registration — `iptv-backend/app/models/user.py`'s `User` model has no `email_verified`/`email_verified_at` column, and `POST /api/auth/register` (`app/routers/auth.py` line 20) activates the account immediately with no verification step (confirmed via grep: zero `email_verified`/`verify_email` references anywhere in `app/`). This also weakens the password-reset flow proposed above — a reset link is only meaningful if the address on file was ever confirmed reachable. Add an `email_verified` boolean + a short-lived verification-token table, send a verification email via the existing `emailer.py` SendGrid helper on registration, add `POST /api/auth/verify-email`, and surface an "unverified" banner with a resend link in `iptv-web/src/views/SettingsView.vue`. Soft-gate only (don't block login on unverified email) to avoid retroactively locking out existing users. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Self-service change-email — `iptv-backend/app/routers/settings.py`'s `GET`/`PUT /api/settings` only reads/writes `UserSettings` (playback/UI preferences); confirmed via grep there is no `PATCH`/`PUT` anywhere in `app/routers/auth.py` or `app/routers/settings.py` that updates `User.email` — a user who needs to correct a typo'd signup email or switch providers has no in-app path today and must be handled manually. Add `PATCH /api/auth/email` (current-password-confirmed; re-triggers the email-verification flow above if that item has landed, otherwise just updates the column) and a matching field in `iptv-web/src/views/SettingsView.vue`'s account section. {cat: backend+web; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] [NEEDS HUMAN/CLAUDE PRODUCT DESIGN — do not decompose to 27B as-is] Referral / invite-a-friend program — confirmed via repo-wide grep, zero `referral`/`invite` functionality exists anywhere in `iptv-backend/app`. This is a standard, high-leverage subscription-app growth lever, but the actual reward mechanics (credit amount/duration, whether it stacks with the existing `trial_days`/`free_trial_days` fields in `app/models/subscription.py`, fraud/self-referral guardrails, and whether to build custom vs. use RevenueCat's own referral/promo tooling since RevenueCat is already the entitlement source of truth here) is a product/business call, not a mechanical coding task. Flagging so this gets a real spec from a human or Claude before any backend/schema work starts — decomposing it into 27B backlog items prematurely risks locking in reward economics nobody actually chose. {cat: backend+web+mobile; size: L; multifile: yes; research: none — flagged for design}

## Research refuel 2026-09-30 (interactive Claude research pass; 9 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Add an SSRF guard for every server-side fetch of user-supplied URLs — iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py `relay_url()` (GET /api/streams/relay, line ~2297) fetches ANY `url` query value with `_http_client.send(..., follow_redirects=True)` for any logged-in user (even free/trial), and `proxy_stream`, `_import_m3u_background` (line ~1130 `client.get(fetch_url)`), `import_xtream` (line ~1695), `app/services/xmltv_parser.py::fetch_xmltv` (line ~106) and `app/services/stream_checker.py` do the same; grep for `ssrf|is_private|ipaddress` in app/ returns nothing. A user can hit Railway-internal hosts, 169.254.169.254 metadata, or localhost. Add `app/services/url_safety.py` (`assert_public_url(url)`: http/https only, resolve host, reject loopback/private/link-local/reserved IPs, re-check on each redirect hop via an httpx event hook), call it at all of those sites, and add tests in tests/ for 127.0.0.1, 10.x, 169.254.169.254, `localhost`, and a redirect-to-private case. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] RevenueCat webhook strips Premium from a paying user the moment they turn off auto-renew, and any unrecognized event (TEST, UNCANCELLATION, NON_RENEWING_PURCHASE, TRANSFER) also downgrades — iptv-backend/app/services/revenuecat.py `parse_webhook_event()` (line ~150) sets `is_active = event_type in ("INITIAL_PURCHASE","RENEWAL","PRODUCT_CHANGE")`, so CANCELLATION (whose own docstring says "may still be active until period end") and every other type map to inactive; app/routers/subscription.py `revenuecat_webhook()` then sets EXPIRED/FREE, `user.is_premium=False` and clears `trial_ends_at` immediately. Per RevenueCat's event docs (https://www.revenuecat.com/docs/integrations/webhooks/event-types-and-fields) CANCELLATION keeps the entitlement until `expiration_at_ms`; only EXPIRATION ends it. Fix: CANCELLATION/UNCANCELLATION keep status ACTIVE (record cancel flag, keep `current_period_end`; revoke early only when `expiration_at_ms` is already past), UNCANCELLATION/NON_RENEWING_PURCHASE activate, TEST/unknown/SUBSCRIBER_ALIAS are no-ops. Existing tests tests/test_subscription_full.py::test_cancellation_removes_premium and tests/test_revenuecat_service.py::test_cancellation_is_active_false enshrine the bug and must be rewritten. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: web}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] DELETE /api/account/me will fail with an FK IntegrityError (HTTP 500) on Postgres for any user who owns streams — iptv-backend/app/services/account.py `delete_account()` (lines 143-187) never deletes the user's `Stream` rows (models/stream.py:37 `user_id ForeignKey("users.id")`, no ondelete), nor `ActiveStreamSession` (models/stream_session.py:15), `EPGSource`+programs (models/epg.py:14), `UserWatchHistory` (string user_id, models/stream.py:112), `ImportRun` (models/import_run.py:23), `DevicePairing` (models/user.py:66). tests/test_account*.py::test_delete_account_removes_user_and_related_data creates a Stream yet passes only because SQLite does not enforce FKs. Apple 5.1.1(v) requires working in-app deletion. Fix: delete dependents of the user's streams first (favorites/watchlist/history/downloads rows by stream_id across ALL users for that user's own streams), then the listed tables, then the user; add a test that runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` on the SQLite test connection (listen on `connect`) so this class of bug is caught. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] The slowapi rate limiter is created but never applied, so login, register, password reset, device-code and feedback are unthrottled — iptv-backend/app/main.py line 68 builds `limiter` and registers the handler (line ~162), but `grep -rn "@limiter\|limiter\." app/` finds zero decorators. Consequences: unlimited credential stuffing on POST /api/auth/login (no lockout either), unlimited reset/verification emails via /api/auth/password/forgot and /resend-verification-email (SendGrid spend + harassment), unlimited 7-day-trial farming via /register, /api/auth/device/approve user_code guessing, and anonymous /api/feedback spam that emails the team inbox. Move the limiter into `app/core/limiter.py` (avoid circular import), apply `@limiter.limit` (e.g. 10/min login, 5/hour forgot, 5/hour register per IP, 20/min device approve/token, 10/hour feedback) with a `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` env toggle, honor X-Forwarded-For behind Railway, add tests asserting 429 after the threshold. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Stop putting the full 8-hour access JWT in playback URLs — iptv-web/src/views/PlayerView.vue line ~88 builds `/api/streams/${id}/proxy?token=<access JWT>`, and iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py `_rewrite_m3u8()` (line ~2044) copies that token into every rewritten segment/key URL, so the account-wide token (ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES default 480, services/auth.py:48) lands in uvicorn/Railway/CDN logs, Referer headers and browser history. Add `POST /api/streams/{id}/playback-token` returning a 15-minute JWT with `type="playback"`, `stream_id` claim; make `get_current_user_or_token_param` accept ONLY playback-type tokens from the `?token=` query (bearer header keeps access tokens) and for /proxy enforce the stream_id match; update PlayerView.vue to fetch it before loading and refresh on expiry; add backend tests (access token in query rejected, wrong stream_id rejected, expired rejected). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Password reset/change cannot revoke existing sessions, and logout is a no-op — iptv-backend/app/routers/auth.py `reset_password()` (line ~190) updates `hashed_password` but every previously issued access (8h) and refresh (30d) JWT stays valid; `logout()` just returns a message and `refresh_token()` never checks anything but signature+is_active, so a stolen refresh token survives a password reset. Add `token_version` int column to models/user.py (+ alembic migration 0010), embed `tv` claim in `AuthService.create_access_token/create_refresh_token`, reject mismatched `tv` in `decode_token` consumers (get_current_user*, refresh), bump it on password reset/change and on logout (and add `POST /api/auth/logout-all`). Tests: old refresh token 401 after reset; fresh login works. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Bound downloaded playlist/EPG size and XML/gzip expansion — iptv-backend/app/services/xmltv_parser.py `fetch_xmltv()` returns `response.content` with no size cap, `parse_xmltv_bytes()` (line ~140) does an unbounded `gz.read()` (gzip bomb) and parses with stdlib `xml.etree.ElementTree` (entity-expansion risk) on a URL the user supplies via the EPG sources router; streams.py `_import_m3u_background` (line ~1130) does `resp.text` with a 120s read timeout and no byte cap, and POST /import /import/m3u accept unbounded raw `content` bodies. On a memory-priced Railway instance one request can OOM the backend. Stream the fetch with a max-bytes limit (env `MAX_PLAYLIST_BYTES`, default 50MB; `MAX_EPG_BYTES`; cap decompressed gzip output via chunked read), switch to `defusedxml.ElementTree` (add to requirements.txt), and return 413/422 with a clear message; add tests with an oversized body and a small gzip that expands past the cap. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] RevenueCat webhook has no idempotency or ordering protection, so a delayed older EXPIRATION can overwrite a newer RENEWAL — iptv-backend/app/routers/subscription.py `revenuecat_webhook()` applies every event blindly and `parse_webhook_event()` (services/revenuecat.py) drops the event's `event_timestamp_ms`/`id`; RevenueCat does not guarantee delivery order and retries on non-2xx. Add `last_event_ms` (BigInteger) to the Subscription model (+ alembic migration), carry `event_timestamp_ms` through `parse_webhook_event`, and ignore events older than `last_event_ms` (return `{"status":"ignored","reason":"stale"}`); add tests for stale-expiration-after-renewal and duplicate delivery. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Expired device-pairing rows and email-verification tokens are never purged — iptv-backend/app/routers/device_auth.py only deletes a `DevicePairing` when the TV happens to poll it after expiry (lines ~137-141), so every abandoned TV code (POST /api/auth/device/code is unauthenticated) persists forever; `grep -rn DevicePairing app/` shows no sweep job, and no job removes expired rows of the verification-token table used by services/auth.py `generate_verification_token`. Add `app/jobs/cleanup_job.py` with a pure `purge_expired_auth_rows(db, now)` function (delete DevicePairing where expires_at < now, expired verification tokens) and register it in app/jobs/health_check.py `start_scheduler()` hourly behind `ENABLE_AUTH_CLEANUP` (default true); unit-test the pure function with expired and live rows. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}

## Auto-research 2026-10-03 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 3 item(s))
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix `check_all_streams` silently dropping health state on gather exceptions — `iptv-backend/app/services/stream_checker.py:138-145`: when `asyncio.gather(return_exceptions=True)` returns an `Exception` for a stream probe, the code only increments `summary["down"]` and logs a warning — it never calls `apply_check_result()`, so `stream.check_failures` is not incremented and `stream.is_active` stays unchanged; the `db.commit()` at line 166 has nothing to flush. A stream that consistently causes OS-level errors (e.g., malformed URL, DNS failure in `aiohttp`) will never be marked inactive by the scheduler. `tests/test_stream_checker.py::TestCheckAllStreams` has three tests; all mock `check_stream` returning dicts, none pass an Exception. Fix: add `apply_check_result(stream, {"is_active": False, "status_code": None, "error": str(check_result), "response_time": None})` inside the `isinstance(check_result, Exception)` branch, then add a test that patches `check_stream` to raise, asserts `stream.is_active` was updated and `db.commit` was awaited. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix `url_safety.assert_public_url` blocking event loop via synchronous `socket.getaddrinfo` — `iptv-backend/app/services/url_safety.py:32`: `socket.getaddrinfo(hostname, None)` is a synchronous DNS call; when the existing `[decomposed]` SSRF-guard wiring item lands and calls this from async endpoints (`epg.py`, `streams.py:relay_url`, `playlist.py`), it will block the entire FastAPI event loop for the full DNS RTT (typically 50–500 ms, sometimes seconds on cold DNS). Fix before wiring: make `assert_public_url` async and replace the `getaddrinfo` call with `await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(None, socket.getaddrinfo, hostname, None)`; update `create_safe_http_client`'s `on_request` hook accordingly; fill in `tests/test_ssrf_guard_integration.py` (currently a 1-line placeholder). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Replace `discover.py` unbounded process-global `_cache` dict with a TTLCache — `iptv-backend/app/routers/discover.py:57`: `_cache: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = {}` has no expiry and is used by `fetch_json()` (lines 157–168) for all iptv-org catalog fetches (categories, channels, streams, countries, logos at lines 49–54); unlike `_trending_cache` and `_personalized_cache` which use `TTLCache(default_ttl=300)`, this dict never evicts — a bad API response or stale catalog is permanently stuck until process restart. Replace with `TTLCache` (6h TTL matches the iptv-org feed's typical update cadence); existing cache keys can stay unchanged. VERIFY: `grep -q "TTLCache" iptv-backend/app/routers/discover.py && ! grep -q "_cache\s*=\s*{}" iptv-backend/app/routers/discover.py`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-04 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 4 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix apply_trial_expiry querying wrong/dead table — `iptv-backend/app/services/premium.py:299` imports `UserWatchHistory` from `app.models.stream` (model at `stream.py:109`, `__tablename__="user_watch_history"`) which is never written to in production; all real watch history goes to `WatchHistory` in `app/models/watch_history.py` (`__tablename__="watch_history"`). Result: the "top-25 most-watched streams" post-trial retention logic (`apply_trial_expiry`, called by `subscription.py:58`) always gets empty `top_ids` and silently falls back to oldest-imported ordering — the feature is broken for every downgrading subscriber. `tests/test_premium_gaps.py:135` writes directly to `UserWatchHistory`, masking this. Fix: replace the import+query to use `WatchHistory` (adapting `user_id` filter from `str` to `int`). VERIFY: new pytest inserts WatchHistory rows, calls `apply_trial_expiry`, and asserts the kept streams are the most-watched ones, not the oldest-imported ones. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wrap email-send failures in auth.py so registration can't leave users in unverifiable limbo — `iptv-backend/app/routers/auth.py:71` calls `await send_email_verification_link(user, token)` with no try/except; if SendGrid is unavailable, the user has already been created in the DB (line 68) and the token already committed, but gets a 500 and cannot log in (soft-gate on email_verified) and doesn't know if registration succeeded. Same gap at lines 261 (resend) and 292 (change-email). Fix: wrap each call in try/except, log the failure, and return a success response with a "verification email will arrive shortly" note rather than propagating the SendGrid exception — matching how other notification send-failures are handled. VERIFY: new pytest mocks `send_email_verification_link` to raise, asserts `POST /auth/register` returns 201 (not 500) and the user row exists. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Wire epg_now_next.pick_now_next() into the now-playing endpoint and eliminate its 2N query-per-request pattern — `iptv-backend/app/services/epg_now_next.py`'s `pick_now_next(programs, at_time)` is fully implemented with zero callers in `app/` (only its own test exercises it); the real `GET /api/epg/now-playing` endpoint (`iptv-backend/app/routers/epg.py:639-664`) fires two separate DB queries per mapped stream (one for current, one for next) inside a Python for-loop — 200 channel mappings = 400 queries per request. Fix: replace the per-stream queries with one bulk load of all relevant EPGPrograms for the user's active source+channel set, then apply `pick_now_next()` per stream in Python, matching the service's intended use case. VERIFY: extend `tests/test_epg_now_playing.py` (or create it) asserting that with N mapped streams the endpoint issues ≤3 DB queries total (not 2N). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Manual research refuel 2026-10-05 round 2 (auto-research blocked: Claude usage limit until Oct 7) - 7 items, each verified against the current overnight/feature checkout: SSRF guard unused, no token revocation, public endpoints without limits, stub route, web nits
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Route the stream proxy and health check through the SSRF guard — `iptv-backend/app/services/url_safety.py` defines `assert_public_url` and `create_safe_http_client()` (rejects private/loopback/link-local targets on every request and redirect hop) but nothing in `app/` calls them (verified by grep), while `StreamCreate.url` (`app/schemas/stream.py:11`) accepts any string and both `proxy_stream` (`app/routers/streams.py:2417`, pooled `_http_client` from line 109) and `check_stream_health` (`streams.py:2191`, plain `httpx.AsyncClient().head(check_url, follow_redirects=True)`) fetch the saved URL directly, so a signed-in user can save `http://169.254.169.254/...` or `http://127.0.0.1:...` and read internal responses. Make `check_stream_health` use `create_safe_http_client()` and report `offline` on `SSRFError`; in `proxy_stream` call `await assert_public_url(stream_url)` before each upstream fetch (keep the pooled client for HLS performance). Add `iptv-backend/tests/test_stream_ssrf.py`: a stream with url `http://127.0.0.1:9/x` makes `/{id}/check` report offline and `/{id}/proxy` fail with no outbound call (monkeypatch `_http_client.get` to fail if called), plus a public-URL case that still proxies. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Make logout and password change actually revoke tokens — `iptv-backend/app/routers/auth.py:174` `logout()` returns a message and does nothing (its docstring: "you might want to blacklist tokens"), and `refresh_token` (`auth.py:131`) issues a new pair without invalidating the old refresh token, so a stolen refresh token stays valid until expiry even after logout or a password reset. Add an integer `token_version` column (default 0) to `User` in `iptv-backend/app/models/user.py` with an Alembic migration in `iptv-backend/alembic/versions/` (UUID/typing conventions as in the existing migrations), embed it as a `tv` claim in `AuthService.create_access_token`/`create_refresh_token` (`app/services/auth.py:78-101`), reject tokens whose `tv` differs from the user's current value in `AuthService.decode_token`'s callers (`get_current_user` and `/refresh`), and bump it in `logout()` and on successful password reset/change. Tokens minted before the migration (no `tv` claim) must be treated as version 0 so existing sessions survive the deploy. Add tests in `iptv-backend/tests/test_token_revocation.py`: after `/logout` the old access and refresh tokens return 401, after `/refresh` the previous refresh token still works only if you decide rotation is out of scope (state the chosen behaviour in the test name), and a pre-migration token without `tv` still authenticates. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-16 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 2 item(s))
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix `GET /api/streams/{stream_id}` returning stale `is_favorite: true` when legacy DB column is set but no per-profile UserFavorite row exists — `iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py:913-914` sets `stream.is_favorite = True` when a `UserFavorite` row is found but has no `else` branch; `favorite_show()` at line 832 still writes `Stream.is_favorite = is_favorite` directly to the DB column (no `UserFavorite` insert), so any stream toggled via the series-view endpoint carries `is_favorite=True` in the DB for all profiles. When `get_stream` is called for a profile that never individually favorited the stream, line 913-914 finds no `UserFavorite` row and leaves `stream.is_favorite` as the stale DB value `True`, so the API returns `"is_favorite": true` falsely. The surrounding code at lines 906-912 already correctly queries `UserFavorite` by `user_id`, `profile_id`, and `stream_id` — the per-profile intent is clear — only the false-reset is missing. Fix: add `else: stream.is_favorite = False` after line 914 so the object always reflects the `UserFavorite` lookup result, overriding any legacy column value. VERIFY: new pytest that seeds a `Stream` row with `is_favorite=True` but no `UserFavorite` row for profile_A, then asserts `GET /api/streams/{stream_id}` (with profile_A header) returns `{"is_favorite": false}`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix `data_retention_job.py` passing deprecated naive `datetime.utcnow()` to the auth-row purge — `iptv-backend/app/jobs/data_retention_job.py:21` calls `cleanup_job.purge_expired_auth_rows(db, datetime.utcnow())` using the Python 3.12-deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, which returns a tz-naive datetime. The roadmap's utcnow sweep (line 142) lists 15 named files but omits this jobs file; confirmed by `grep -n "utcnow" iptv-backend/app/jobs/data_retention_job.py` → line 21 only. `app/services/auth.py` uses `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` throughout for all auth-token expiry comparisons, so `expires_at` values stored in Postgres are tz-aware (`TIMESTAMPTZ`); passing a naive cutoff creates an ambiguous comparison that Postgres may raise as a TypeError or silently misinterpret, causing the purge to over-delete or under-delete expired device-pairing and email-verification tokens. Fix: change line 21 to `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` and extend the import at line 10 from `from datetime import datetime` to `from datetime import datetime, timezone`. VERIFY: `grep -n "utcnow" iptv-backend/app/jobs/data_retention_job.py` returns nothing; `pytest iptv-backend/tests/ -q` passes unchanged. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-16 04:26 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens type-safety; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot b25c45ae9f, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix _extract_year to return correct 4-digit year from title — `iptv-backend/app/services/tmdb.py:261` (`_extract_year`) The regex group (19|20) captures only the first two digits, so match.group() returns '19' or '20' instead of the full year like '1995'. This causes TMDB search to use year=19 or year=20, which matches no content and breaks movie identification. Evidence: `match = re.search(r'\b(19|20)\d{2}\b', title) if match: return int(match.group())`. Fix: Change return int(match.group()) to return int(match.group(0)) to capture the entire matched string (the 4-digit year). Add iptv-backend/tests/test_tmdb_extract_year.py with a test that calls _extract_year('Movie (1995)') and asserts it returns 1995. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_tmdb_extract_year.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix relay endpoint not forwarding Range header for VOD byte-range seeking — `iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py:2721` relay endpoint builds `upstream_headers = PROXY_HEADERS.copy()` but never forwards the client `Range` header, unlike `proxy_stream` which explicitly does so at lines 2540-2543. For VOD MP4 content rewritten through the relay, players send Range requests to seek; relay sends full-file requests to upstream, returning status 200 instead of 206 and breaking seek. Fix: add `range_hdr = (request.headers.get("range") or request.headers.get("Range")); upstream_headers["Range"] = range_hdr` after the Host header block in relay (line ~2743). VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python -m pytest tests/test_relay_url_behavior.py -v` {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add tests for get_trending_streams region filter and day-window — `iptv-backend/app/services/trending.py` has real filtering logic (created_at day window + region) with zero test coverage (no `test_trending*` file exists); the broken region filter (exact group equality) is only visible under test. Create `iptv-backend/tests/test_trending_service.py` with 3 tests: (1) region=None returns all active streams in window, (2) region="US" matches streams whose group contains a US token (not exact equality) confirming the bug and fix, (3) streams outside the day window are excluded. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python -m pytest tests/test_trending_service.py -v` {cat: test; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-19 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 4 item(s))
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `GET /api/content/continue-watching` bypasses kid-profile parental rating ceiling — `iptv-backend/app/routers/content.py:463`'s `get_continue_watching()` has no `active_profile` dependency and no `profile_rating_ceiling()`/`MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` filter, unlike the sibling `get_vod_catalog()` at line 629 which applies both. `ContentProgress` (models/content.py:116) has only `user_id`, no `profile_id`, so ALL profiles on a family premium account share one progress list — a kid profile sees every TV-MA title a parent started, proactively surfaced in their Continue Watching rail. Fix: add `active_profile: Optional[Profile] = Depends(get_active_profile)` to `get_continue_watching`, filter `stream_map` using `vod_visible_query` ceiling logic after line 510, and drop result items with no remaining `available_streams`. VERIFY: new pytest in `iptv-backend/tests/test_continue_watching_batching.py` asserting a kid-ceiling profile's endpoint never returns content whose only associated stream matches `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none} {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Android `EpgViewModel.toggleChannelFavorite()` uses legacy account-wide `is_favorite` API, surviving the decomposed Android Favorites fix — `EpgViewModel.kt:146` calls `api.updateStream(channel.streamId, StreamUpdate(isFavorite = newValue))` (the per-stream, account-scoped flag), not the profile-scoped `POST /api/favorites`. The [decomposed] Android Favorites profile-scoping item creates `FavoritesRepository.kt` and updates `StreamsViewModel.toggleFavorite()` but explicitly names only `FavoritesScreen`/`StreamsListView` — leaving this separate EPG code path intact. `EpgViewModelTest.kt:491+505` tests only verify optimistic-flip behavior, not which endpoint is called. Fix: after `FavoritesRepository.kt` exists, update `toggleChannelFavorite()` in `EpgViewModel.kt` to call `favoritesRepository.addFavorite()`/`removeFavorite()` and add a test asserting the `/api/favorites` URL is called instead of `/api/streams/{id}`. VERIFY: `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests "*EpgViewModelTest*"` passes with new assertion on endpoint URL. {cat: android; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] iOS `MainTabView.swift` tab items have no `.accessibilityIdentifier(...)`, forcing E2E tab navigation to rely on localized label strings — `grep accessibilityIdentifier ChickadeeStreams/Views/MainTabView.swift` returns 0 hits; `AppNavigation.swift:73` matches tabs via `tab.rawValue` (e.g. `"Streams"`, `"Discover"` — localized, rename-fragile). `AppNavigation.swift:10` has an explicit `TODO(app): add .accessibilityIdentifier("tab_<name>") to each .tabItem in MainTabView` citing the fragility. Confirmed gap: localization or label rename silently breaks every tab-navigation E2E test without a compile error, and VoiceOver users also lose predictable identifier-based navigation. Fix: add `.accessibilityIdentifier("tab_streams")` etc. to each `.tabItem` in `MainTabView.swift`; update `AppNavigation.swift:73-86` to prefer identifier-based lookup (falling back to `tab.rawValue` for backward compatibility). Files: `iptv-ios/ChickadeeStreams/Views/MainTabView.swift`, `ChickadeeStreamsUITests/Pages/AppNavigation.swift`. VERIFY: `AppNavigation.select(.discover)` in any UITest finds the tab via identifier, confirmed by XCUITest run. {cat: ios; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `downloads.py`'s `_visible_stream_or_404` skips parental rating ceiling — `iptv-backend/app/routers/downloads.py:54`'s helper only checks stream ownership (`Stream.user_id == user.id | is_global`), never `profile_rating_ceiling()`/`MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS`. `create_download()` at line 98 already receives `active_profile: Optional[Profile] = Depends(get_active_profile)` (line 103) but never gates on the profile's rating ceiling before inserting the `Download` row — confirmed by `grep -n "MATURE_CATEGORY\|profile_rating" downloads.py` returning 0 hits. A kid profile on a family premium account can POST `{"stream_id": <known mature VOD id>}` directly and download it, bypassing the browse-time filter that `vod_visible_query` applies. Same class of bypass as the already-fixed search.py and decomposed VOD/discovery endpoints. Fix: after resolving `stream`, check `profile_rating_ceiling(db, current_user.id, active_profile)` and raise 403 if the stream matches `MATURE_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS`. VERIFY: new pytest in `iptv-backend/tests/test_downloads.py` asserting `POST /api/downloads` with a `TV-MA`-category stream returns 403 for a kid-ceiling profile. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `GET /api/history/continue` (watch_history.py) exposes mature content to kid profiles — `get_continue_watching` at `watch_history.py:507-521` queries `ContinueWatching` filtered by profile but never applies `profile_rating_ceiling`/`stream_within_ceiling` from `parental_controls.py`. Every other listing surface (search.py:84-91, vod_visible_query in content.py:568-573) enforces the kid ceiling; this endpoint is the gap. Fix: after the join, filter out streams where `stream_within_ceiling` returns False for the active profile's ceiling. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Discovery continue-watching recommendations not profile-scoped — `stream_discovery_service.py:424-428` queries `ContinueWatching.user_id == user_id` with no `profile_id` predicate, mixing every profile's in-progress content into a single list. On multi-profile accounts a kid profile's discovery rail receives the adult profile's viewing progress (and vice versa). Fix: thread `profile_id` into `get_recommendations_continue_watching` (line 399) and add the filter to the SQLAlchemy `select` at line 427. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `SearchHistory` model lacks `profile_id` — `models/search.py:14-28` defines `SearchHistory` with only `user_id`. The `search_history` endpoint (`search.py:141-165`) and `search_suggestions` (`search.py:184-239`) query by `user_id` only, so a kid profile sees the parent's mature search queries as suggestions and the parent sees the kid's searches. Fix requires an Alembic migration adding a nullable `profile_id` FK column to `search_history`, and updating both endpoints to filter by `active_profile_id(active_profile)`, matching the pattern used by `WatchHistory` and `ContinueWatching`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `update_reminder` accepts past `program_start` dates — `reminders.py:152-181` calls `setattr(reminder, field, value)` with no temporal validation, so a user can silently push a pending reminder's fire-time into the past. `create_reminder` at line 81-84 correctly rejects past dates; the PATCH path bypasses that gate entirely, leaving the reminder stranded in a state that will never transition to "notified" via `get_due_reminders`. Fix: apply the same `program_start < now` check in `update_reminder` before persisting. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

A kid profile that started a mature-rated VOD (before rating enforcement existed, or via a content-type mismatch) will see it in the Continue Watching strip. This item has been escalated as a `[CLAUDE]` item after 4 failed fleet attempts — the blast radius note in recent.txt says the 27B keeps re-guessing without diagnosis. Diagnosis: `get_continue_watching` must (1) call `profile_rating_ceiling(db, current_user.id, active_profile)` then (2) pass the ceiling to `filter_by_rating_ceiling(items, ceiling)` before returning. The helpers exist and are tested; only the wiring call is missing. - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Fix open redirect vulnerability in LoginView — `iptv-web/src/views/LoginView.vue:25` (`handleSubmit`) The redirect query parameter is cast to a string and assigned directly to window.location.href without validating that it is a relative URL or an allowed domain, allowing an attacker to craft a login URL that redirects the user to a malicious site after successful authentication. Evidence: `const redirect = route.query.redirect as string if (redirect) { window.location.href = redirect }`. Fix: Validate that the redirect value starts with '/' and does not start with '//' before using it for navigation. Add iptv-web/src/views/LoginView.test.ts to verify that external URLs are blocked. ## Qwen explore 2026-10-23 02:13 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens test-gaps; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot f0a6079acc, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] LinkTvView onInput shifts digits into the letter slot when 5+ letters are typed — `iptv-web/src/views/LinkTvView.vue:29` (`onInput`) digits are sliced from the raw string at fixed positions 4-7 before letters are filtered, so typing five or more letters (e.g. "ABCDE1234") puts a letter into the digit slot, which is then stripped, yielding "ABCD" with no digits and an invalid code even though four valid digits were entered. Evidence: `const raw = (e.target as HTMLInputElement).value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z0-9]/g, '') const letters = raw.slice(0, 4).replace(/[^A-Z]/g, '') const`. Fix: Derive letters and digits by character class rather than fixed position: take the first four A-Z characters as letters and the first four 0-9 characters as digits (e.g. letters = raw.match(/[A-Z]/g)?.slice(0,4) joined, digits = raw.match(/[0-9]/g)?.slice(0,4) joined). Add src/views/LinkTvView.test.ts. VERIFY: `cd iptv-web && npx vitest run src/views/LinkTvView.test.ts`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-24 22:06 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens a11y-ux; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 455d08fbdd, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add aria-labels to icon-only profile edit and delete buttons — `iptv-web/src/views/ProfilesView.vue:177` (`template`) The edit and delete profile buttons contain only icons with no aria-label, so screen readers announce them as generic "button" with no context, violating WCAG 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value. Evidence: `<button type="button" data-testid="edit-profile" class="text-gray-400 hover:text-primary-600" @click.stop="openEdit(p)" > <PencilSquareIcon class="h-4`. Fix: Add :aria-label="'Edit ${p.name}'" to the edit button and :aria-label="'Delete ${p.name}'" to the delete button. Add iptv-web/src/views/ProfilesView.test.ts to verify accessible names. VERIFY: `cd iptv-web && npx vitest run src/views/ProfilesView.test.ts`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-25 00:53 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens authz-input; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot b31808f460, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix rate limit IP spoofing via X-Forwarded-For — `iptv-backend/app/core/limiter.py:31` (`get_remote_address`) When TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS is not set, the function trusts the first entry in the X-Forwarded-For header, which is fully controlled by the client. This allows any user to bypass rate limits by rotating the X-Forwarded-For value. Evidence: `# Default (TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS unset): the first IP in the list is the original client return parts[0] if parts else "unknown"`. Fix: Change the default behavior to ignore X-Forwarded-For when TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS is not explicitly set to a positive integer, falling back to request.client.host instead. Add iptv-backend/tests/test_limiter_spoofing.py to verify that spoofed headers do not change the rate limit key. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_limiter_spoofing.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-25 08:10 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 6590ab275d, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix N+1 device token query in flush_pending — `iptv-backend/app/services/notifications.py:196` (`flush_pending`) The function executes a separate database query for every pending notification row to fetch device tokens, resulting in O(N) queries where N is the number of pending notifications (up to 100). This causes significant latency and database load as the queue grows. Evidence: `tokens = ( db.query(DeviceToken) .filter(DeviceToken.user_id == notif.user_id) .all() )`. Fix: Hoist the device token lookup out of the loop by fetching all tokens for the unique user IDs in the pending batch in a single query, then group them by user_id in Python. Add iptv-backend/tests/test_notifications_flush.py to verify that flush_pending executes only one DeviceToken query for multiple users. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_notifications_flush.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-26 13:57 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens authz-input; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 9b43cf2daf, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix TTLCache.get_or_set to handle None cached values — `iptv-backend/app/services/cache.py:46` (`TTLCache`) When a producer returns None and it is cached, subsequent calls to get_or_set will re-execute the producer because 'cached is not None' evaluates to False for None values, defeating the cache purpose for empty results. Evidence: `cached = self.get(key) if cached is not None: return cached value = producer() self.set(key, value, ttl)`. Fix: Change the check to distinguish between 'not in cache' and 'cached None'. Use a sentinel or check 'if key in self._store' instead of relying on the return value. Add iptv-backend/tests/test_cache_none_handling.py to verify None values are cached correctly. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_cache_none_handling.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-27 17:07 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 06447ff21c, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Polling loop in SourcesView makes redundant full-source fetches — `iptv-web/src/views/SourcesView.vue:129` (`pollForNewRun`) The polling loop calls reload() every 2 seconds for up to 120 seconds (60 attempts), and reload() fetches ALL playlist sources AND ALL EPG sources from the backend each time. For a user with many sources, this generates up to 60 unnecessary full-list API calls per refresh operation, straining the backend and wasting bandwidth. Evidence: `for (let i = 0; i < POLL_MAX_ATTEMPTS && !disposed; i++) { await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, POLL_INTERVAL_MS)) try { await reload()`. Fix: Replace the full reload() with a targeted poll endpoint that only fetches the status of the specific source being refreshed (e.g., GET /api/streams/sources/{id}/runs?limit=1 or GET /api/epg/sources/{id}/runs?limit=1). Add iptv-web/src/views/SourcesView.test.ts to verify pollForNewRun calls the targeted endpoint instead of reload(). VERIFY: `cd iptv-web && npx vitest run src/views/SourcesView.test.ts`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: web; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

Add a pure function `find_lapsed_subscriptions(db, now, grace_days)` + `downgrade_lapsed_subscriptions(db, now)` in a NEW `iptv-backend/app/services/subscription_downgrade.py` (grace via env `DOWNGRADE_GRACE_DAYS`, default 3) that sets `status=expired`, `tier=free` and `User.is_premium=False` for premium `Subscription` rows (models in `app/models/subscription.py`) whose `current_period_end` + grace is in the past, skips rows with NULL `current_period_end`, logs one line per transition, and NEVER touches rows with an in-flight trial (`User.trial_ends_at` in the future). Add a thin job `iptv-backend/app/jobs/downgrade_job.py` mirroring `app/jobs/win_back_job.py` and schedule it daily in `iptv-backend/app/jobs/health_check.py` `start_scheduler` behind env `ENABLE_DOWNGRADE_JOB` (default true), next to the win-back job registration. ## Qwen explore 2026-10-31 05:57 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 1d810870d4, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix N+1 queries in detach_streams_from_groups loop — `iptv-backend/app/services/content_dedup.py:139` (`detach_streams_from_groups`) The function executes 2-3 separate database queries for every group ID in the 'gids' set, which is derived from the number of distinct groups containing the deleted streams. For a bulk deletion involving many groups, this results in O(N) round-trips to the database instead of a single batched operation. Evidence: `for gid in gids: group = db.query(ContentGroup).filter(ContentGroup.id == gid).first() if group is None: continue remaining =`. Fix: Replace the per-group loop with batched queries: fetch all 'ContentGroup' objects for 'gids' in one query, and calculate remaining member counts for all 'gids' in a single grouped query. Add iptv-backend/tests/test_content_dedup_batch.py to verify that 'detach_streams_from_groups' performs a constant number of database queries regardless of the number of groups. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_content_dedup_batch.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-11-01 02:17 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 042bfdc07d, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Eliminate N+1 user lookups in win-back email dispatch — `iptv-backend/app/services/win_back.py:67` (`dispatch_win_back_emails`) The function executes a separate database query to fetch the User for each subscription candidate. If there are many candidates (e.g., after a mass expiration), this results in N+1 queries, significantly increasing database load and latency. Evidence: `for sub in candidates: user = db.query(User).filter(User.id == sub.user_id).first() if not user: continue`. Fix: Fetch all required users in a single query using 'db.query(User).filter(User.id.in_([sub.user_id for sub in candidates])).all()' before the loop, and look up users from a dictionary in the loop. Add iptv-backend/tests/test_win_back_n_plus_one.py to verify that only one user query is executed. VERIFY: `cd iptv-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_win_back_n_plus_one.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Seeded round 3 2026-11-01 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Win-back must only mark `win_back_sent_at` when the email actually sent — `iptv-backend/app/services/win_back.py` `dispatch_win_back_emails` awaits `send_win_back_email(...)` and ignores its bool result, then sets `sub.win_back_sent_at = now` and counts it; `emailer.send_email` returns False when `SENDGRID_API_KEY` is unset or SendGrid errors, so every candidate is permanently marked sent without an email and never retried. Only stamp and count on a True return; leave the row unstamped on False. Tests in `tests/test_win_back.py`-style new file using a monkeypatched sender. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Cap win-back emails per sweep — `dispatch_win_back_emails` in `iptv-backend/app/services/win_back.py` sends to every candidate in one run (a mass expiration could blast thousands of emails and a long-held DB session). Add env `WIN_BACK_MAX_PER_RUN` (default 200), process oldest-inactive first, leave the remainder for the next sweep (they stay unstamped). No schema change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Expire stale pending push notifications instead of delivering them days late — `flush_pending` in `iptv-backend/app/services/notifications.py` delivers any `QueuedNotification` with status `pending` however old (e.g. a "reminder" queued while push creds were unset for a week). Add env `NOTIFICATION_PENDING_MAX_AGE_HOURS` (default 48); older pending rows are set to status `expired` (status is a free String(20), no migration) with `error="stale"`, counted in a new `expired` key of the returned summary (update `notifications_job.notification_flush_job`'s default summary dict). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Per-user daily cap on queued push notifications — `enqueue_notification` in `iptv-backend/app/services/notifications.py` honours the per-kind preference and an optional dedupe window but has no ceiling per user. Add env `NOTIFICATION_MAX_PER_USER_PER_DAY` (default 20): when the user already has that many `QueuedNotification` rows created in the last 24h (any kind), return None and log at info. Tests cover under cap, at cap, other user unaffected. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Prune delivered/old `QueuedNotification` rows in the daily retention job — nothing deletes `queued_notifications` (only account deletion does, `services/account.py:213`), so the table grows forever. Add `purge_old_notifications(db, now, days)` to `iptv-backend/app/jobs/cleanup_job.py` deleting rows with status in (sent, skipped, failed, expired) older than `NOTIFICATION_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 30; never touch `pending`), and call it from `_run()` in `iptv-backend/app/jobs/data_retention_job.py` (it is not user content, so it is not opt-in like watch history). Async session, tz-aware `now`. Tests alongside `tests/test_cleanup_job.py`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Cap pending EPG reminders per user — `create_reminder` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/reminders.py` has only a 30/minute rate limit, so one account can accumulate unbounded `pending` `EPGReminder` rows that `find_due_reminders` then scans every run. Add env `MAX_PENDING_REMINDERS_PER_USER` (default 200); when the user already has that many `status == "pending"` reminders return HTTP 409 with a clear detail. Tests: at cap 409, cancelled/notified rows do not count, other user unaffected. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Narrow `find_due_reminders` to a SQL time window instead of loading every pending reminder — `iptv-backend/app/services/reminders.py` does `q.all()` over all `status == "pending"` rows and filters fire time in Python each scheduler run. Add a SQL prefilter (`program_start <= now + max allowed remind_before_minutes` and `program_start >= now - 30 minutes`, using the max from the reminder request schema) and keep the Python `compute_reminder_fire_time` check as the exact filter. Behaviour must be identical; add a test with far-future and long-past reminders confirming they are excluded and boundary ones included. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Import run counts must not include the batch that was rolled back — in `iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py` playlist import (BATCH=500 loop, around lines 1488-1575) `created_count`/`updated_count`/`skipped_count` are incremented before each `db.commit()`; when a later batch raises, `db.rollback()` undoes only the failing batch yet the failed `ImportRun` records counts that include it. Track counts per batch and add them to the totals only after a successful commit, so the failed run reports exactly what was committed. Test by forcing the second batch to raise (monkeypatch) and asserting the `ImportRun` counts equal the first batch only. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Large-playlist parse test at 100k entries with capped error details — `parse_m3u_content` (`iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py:1201`) records dropped rows with 1-based line numbers into an `ErrorCollector` (`services/import_errors.py`, `MAX_ERROR_DETAILS = 20`). - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Cap push device tokens per user — `register_device_token` in `iptv-backend/app/services/notifications.py` inserts a new `DeviceToken` for every new token string, and `POST /api/notifications/devices` is only rate-limited (20/minute), so stale tokens from reinstalls accumulate and every notification fans out to all of them.
