## Seeded round 5 2026-11-04 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] A `null` in `PUT /api/settings` permanently breaks the user's settings endpoints (500 on every later GET/PUT) — `UserSettingsUpdate` in `iptv-backend/app/schemas/settings.py` types every field `X | None = None`, and `update_settings` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/settings.py` does `settings_data.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)` then `setattr(settings, field, value)`, so `{"theme": null, "autoplay": null}` writes NULL into the nullable `user_settings` columns, the PUT itself dies with `ResponseValidationError`, and every later `GET /api/settings` also fails because `UserSettingsResponse` requires non-null bool/Literal values (probe confirmed: row stored as `None, None`, GET raises). Fix: in `update_settings` drop keys whose value is `None` (`{k: v for k, v in update_data.items() if v is not None}`) so an explicit null means "leave unchanged"; no schema or migration change. VERIFY: NEW `iptv-backend/tests/test_settings_null_update.py` PUTs `{"theme": null, "autoplay": null, "default_quality": null}` via `authenticated_client`, asserts 200 with defaults intact, then GET `/api/settings` is 200 and a follow-up `{"theme": "dark"}` PUT still works: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_settings_null_update.py tests/test_settings_update_valid.py tests/test_settings_router.py tests/test_settings_full.py -q`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `PUT /api/history/{stream_id}` 500s on the first progress write that carries `watch_duration`, and accepts negative durations that corrupt watch-time totals — `update_watch_progress` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/watch_history.py` (line ~279) creates a new `WatchHistory(...)` and immediately runs `history.total_watch_time_seconds += update_data.watch_duration`, but the Column default (`Float, default=0`) is only applied at flush so the attribute is still `None` (probe: `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for +=: 'NoneType' and 'float'`); and `WatchHistoryUpdate.watch_duration` / `.duration_seconds` in `iptv-backend/app/schemas/watch_history.py` (plus `ContinueWatchingCreate/Update.duration_seconds`) have no `ge=0`, so `watch_duration: -5000` stores `total_watch_time_seconds = -5000` (negative watch hours in `/api/history/stats` and analytics). Fix: add `Field(default=None, ge=0)` to those duration fields, and in the handler use `history.total_watch_time_seconds = (history.total_watch_time_seconds or 0) + update_data.watch_duration`. VERIFY: NEW `iptv-backend/tests/test_watch_history_put_duration.py` asserts a first `PUT /api/history/{id}` with `{"progress_seconds": 10, "watch_duration": 30}` returns 200 with `total_watch_time_seconds == 30`, a second adds correctly, and `watch_duration: -5` / `duration_seconds: -1` return 422: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_watch_history_put_duration.py tests/test_watch_history_full.py tests/test_watch_history_update_progress_branches.py tests/test_watch_history_schemas.py tests/test_watch_history_validation.py -q`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `PUT /api/streams/{id}` with a `null` field corrupts the stream or 500s — `update_stream` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/streams.py` (line ~1125) `setattr`s every `exclude_unset` key from `StreamUpdate` (all `Optional`), so `{"url": null}` raises `IntegrityError` (`streams.url` NOT NULL, HTTP 500), `{"is_favorite": null}` stores NULL and every later read of that stream fails `StreamResponse` validation (probe: `ResponseValidationError`), and `{"name": null}` / `{"is_active": null}` / `{"stream_type": null}` silently blank the field (a NULL `is_active` also hides the stream from every `is_active == True` list). Fix: in `update_stream` skip `None` values for the non-clearable fields `name`, `url`, `is_active`, `is_favorite`, `stream_type`, `sort_order`, `fallback_urls` (keep `None` meaning "clear" for `logo_url`, `category`, `group`, `username`, `password`, `epg_channel_id`). VERIFY: NEW `iptv-backend/tests/test_stream_update_nulls.py` creates a stream via POST, PUTs each of `{"url": null}`, `{"is_favorite": null}`, `{"name": null}`, `{"is_active": null}` and asserts 200, the original values unchanged and `GET /api/streams/{id}` still 200, while `{"logo_url": null}` still clears: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_stream_update_nulls.py tests/test_streams_crud.py -q`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Profile `is_kids_mode` is accepted by the API but never stored or returned — `ProfileCreate`/`ProfileUpdate`/`ProfileResponse` in `iptv-backend/app/schemas/profile.py` all declare `is_kids_mode`, yet `create_profile` builds `Profile(... is_kid=body.is_kid ...)`, `update_profile` only applies `body.is_kid`, and `_to_response` (all in `iptv-backend/app/routers/profiles.py`) omits it, so the column `Profile.is_kids_mode` (read by `AuthService`/login `is_kids_mode` flag in `app/services/subscription.py:186`) can never be set through the API and the response always says `false` (probe: POST `{"is_kids_mode": true}` -> 201 with `is_kids_mode: false`, DB row False; PATCH likewise). Fix: pass `is_kids_mode=body.is_kids_mode` in `create_profile`, add `if body.is_kids_mode is not None: profile.is_kids_mode = body.is_kids_mode` in `update_profile`, and return `is_kids_mode=bool(profile.is_kids_mode)` from `_to_response`. VERIFY: NEW `iptv-backend/tests/test_profiles_kids_mode.py` POSTs `{"name": "Kid", "is_kid": true, "is_kids_mode": true}` and asserts the response and DB row are True, PATCHes it to false and asserts both flip, and that login for that user reports `is_kids_mode` accordingly: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_profiles_kids_mode.py tests/test_profiles_create.py tests/test_profiles_update.py tests/test_routers_auth.py -q`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `PATCH /api/epg/reminders/{id}` 500s on explicit nulls — `ReminderUpdate` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/reminders.py` types `program_title`, `program_start` and `remind_before_minutes` as `Optional[...] = None`, and `update_reminder` applies `body.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)` with `setattr`, so `{"program_title": null}`, `{"remind_before_minutes": null}` or `{"program_start": null}` hit the NOT NULL columns of `epg_reminders` and raise `IntegrityError` (probe confirmed all three; unhandled 500). Fix: in `update_reminder` drop `None` values for those three non-nullable fields before the `setattr` loop (explicit null = leave unchanged; `channel_id`/`stream_id` keep clear-on-null). - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `PUT /api/parental/settings` accepts nulls (settings row poisoned, every later call 500s) and any string as a rating — `ParentalSettingsUpdate` in `iptv-backend/app/schemas/parental.py` makes `restrictions_enabled`, `max_rating`, `blocked_categories`, `lock_settings`, `kid_max_rating` etc. `Optional`, and `update_parental_settings` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/parental.py` (line ~336) `setattr`s each `exclude_unset` value, so `{"restrictions_enabled": null}` persists NULL and `ParentalSettingsResponse` then fails validation on this and every later GET/PUT (probe: `ResponseValidationError`); separately `max_rating`/`kid_max_rating` accept `"banana"` or `"tv-14"` (case-sensitive lookup in `allowed_ratings_for_ceiling`, `app/services/parental_controls.py`, silently falls back to TV-PG). Fix: drop `None` values in the handler loop, and add a `field_validator("max_rating", "kid_max_rating")` on `ParentalSettingsUpdate` that upper-cases/strips and rejects values not in `app.services.parental_rating_gate.RATING_HIERARCHY` (422). - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] EPG source create/update has no field validation and `PUT /api/epg/sources/{id}` 500s on `{"name": null}` — `EPGSourceBase`/`EPGSourceUpdate` in `iptv-backend/app/schemas/epg.py` use bare `name: str` / `Optional[str]` and unbounded `update_frequency_hours: int`, and `update_epg_source` in `iptv-backend/app/routers/epg.py` (line ~603) `setattr`s all `exclude_unset` keys, so `{"name": null}` raises `IntegrityError` (NOT NULL), `{"name": ""}` and a 400-char name (column is `String(255)`, fails on Postgres) are accepted, and `update_frequency_hours` of `0` or `-5` is accepted (probe confirmed each). ## Seeded round 8 2026-11-09 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Programme times with a colon offset (`+05:30`) or `Z`/`UTC` are parsed as UTC, shifting every airing by the offset, and `Z` logs a traceback per programme - `_parse_xmltv_timestamp` in `iptv-backend/app/services/xmltv_parser.py` does `tz_str = tz_part.lstrip("+-")` then `int(tz_str[2:4])`, so `+05:30` gives `int(":3")` -> ValueError -> `logger.exception` and NO offset applied (probe: `"20240115143000 +05:30"` returns 14:30 UTC, correct is 09:00 UTC; `"20240115143000 Z"` returns 14:30 but logs a ValueError traceback). Fix: before the sign/hours parse, set `tz_part = tz_part.strip()`; if `tz_part.upper() in ("Z", "UTC", "GMT")` use `"+0000"`; otherwise `tz_part = tz_part.replace(":", "")`; the `+0530`/`-0500` paths are unchanged. VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_xmltv_timestamp_edgecases.py`: `+05:30` -> `datetime(2024,1,15,9,0,tzinfo=timezone.utc)`, `-03:30` -> 18:00 UTC, `Z` and `UTC` -> 14:30 UTC with `caplog` containing no "Failed to parse timezone offset", `+0530` still 09:00: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_xmltv_timestamp_edgecases.py -q` and the new colon/Z cases fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Quality-tag stripping glues words together and eats letters inside real titles - `cleanDisplayTitle` in `iptv-web/src/utils/cleanDisplayTitle.ts` builds `new RegExp(`([._\\s-])?${tag}([._\\s-])?`, 'gi')` with no word boundary and removes the separators on BOTH sides, so `Isaac Asimov` becomes `IsAsimov` (the `aac` tag), `Movie 1080p Extended` becomes `MovieExtended`, `Planet 4K Special` becomes `PlanetSpecial` and `Mad Max 24k Gold` becomes `Mad Max 2Gold` (probe confirmed all four). Fix: match the tag only when not embedded in an alphanumeric run, i.e. `new RegExp(`(?<![A-Za-z0-9])${tag}(?![A-Za-z0-9])`, 'gi')`, and replace each match with a single space (the existing dot/underscore collapse, whitespace collapse and trim at the end already clean up; keep the year/bracket trimming untouched). VERIFY: add cases to `iptv-web/src/utils/__tests__/cleanDisplayTitle.test.ts`: `Isaac Asimov` -> `Isaac Asimov`, `Movie 1080p Extended` -> `Movie Extended`, `Planet 4K Special` -> `Planet Special`, `Mad Max 24k Gold` -> `Mad Max 24k Gold`, `Movie.720p.x264` -> `Movie` (the existing cases must still pass): `cd iptv-web && npx vitest run src/utils/__tests__/cleanDisplayTitle.test.ts src/utils/__tests__/cleanDisplayTitle.spec.ts` and the first four new cases fail before the change. {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Out-of-order search responses overwrite newer results - `search` in `iptv-web/src/stores/search.ts` awaits `api.searchStreams(term, opts)` and unconditionally assigns `results`/`total`/`error`/`loading`, so when "ab" is slow and "abc" returns first, the late "ab" response replaces the "abc" results while `query` still says "abc" (probe with a deferred first call: `results` ends as the "ab" rows). `fetchSuggestions` has the same race. Fix: add a module-closure `let searchSeq = 0` / `let suggestSeq = 0`; each call does `const seq = ++searchSeq`, and after the await (in the try, the catch and the finally) bails out when `seq !== searchSeq` (only the newest call may write `results`, `total`, `error`, `loading=false`; an empty-term call also bumps the counter so it cancels an in-flight search); same pattern for suggestions. VERIFY: add to `iptv-web/src/stores/__tests__/search.test.ts`: first `searchStreams` call returns a deferred promise, second resolves `{results:[{id:2}],total:1}`, `await store.search('abc')` then resolve the first with `{results:[{id:1}],total:1}` and await it -> `store.results` equals `[{id:2}]`; a stale first call that REJECTS afterwards leaves `store.error` null; same two cases for `fetchSuggestions`: `cd iptv-web && npx vitest run src/stores/__tests__/search.test.ts src/stores/__tests__/search.clear.test.ts` and the stale cases fail before the change. {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] A resolution in a title (`1920x1080`) or a name ending in `s3E4` is parsed as a TV episode - `parse_episode_identifier` in `iptv-backend/app/services/tmdb.py` (line ~19) uses unanchored `S(\d+)\s*E(\d+)` and `(\d+)x(\d+)`, so `Movie 1920x1080` returns `(1920, 1080)` and `Ross3E4 Special` returns `(3, 4)` (probe confirmed), which makes `group_key` in `content_dedup.py` treat movies as episodes and mis-groups them. Fix: `r'\bS(\d{1,4})\s*E(\d{1,4})\b'`, `r'\b(\d{1,2})x(\d{2,3})\b'` (still case-insensitive); the `Season N ... Episode M` branch is unchanged. VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_tmdb.py` (next to `test_1x02_format`): `parse_episode_identifier("Movie 1920x1080") is None`, `("Ross3E4 Special") is None`, `("Show 1x02") == (1, 2)`, `("Show.S01E02.720p") == (1, 2)`, `("Show 12x105") == (12, 105)`: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_tmdb.py -q` and the first two new assertions fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Dotted release names never get a year, so `Dune.2021.1080p` and `Dune (2021)` fall into different dedup groups - `_YEAR_TRAIL_RE` in `iptv-backend/app/services/content_dedup.py` is `\s((?:19|20)\d{2})\s*$` and needs whitespace before the year, so `normalize_title("Dune.2021.1080p")` returns `("dune 2021", None)` (probe) while `normalize_title("Dune (2021)")` returns `("dune", 2021)`. Fix: change it to `[\s._]((?:19|20)\d{2})[\s._]*$` (a bare `1917` or `2012` title with no preceding separator must still be a title, not a year). VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_content_dedup.py` (the existing `normalize_title` cases): `normalize_title("Dune.2021.1080p") == ("dune", 2021)`, `normalize_title("The.Matrix.1999") == ("the matrix", 1999)`, `normalize_title("1917") == ("1917", None)`, `normalize_title("2012") == ("2012", None)`, `normalize_title("Blade Runner 2049") == ("blade runner", 2049)`: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_content_dedup.py -q` and the two dotted cases fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none} [small-decomposition n=2]
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] IPv6 playlist links are corrupted by the duplicate-link normaliser - `normalize_link` in `iptv-backend/app/services/url_normalize.py` rebuilds the netloc from `parts.hostname` (brackets stripped) so `http://[::1]:8080/x/` becomes `http://::1:8080/x`, an unparseable address, and two different IPv6 sources can collide or never match their stored form (probe confirmed). Fix: when the lowercased host contains `:` wrap it back as `[host]` before adding `:port`/credentials; everything else unchanged. VERIFY: NEW `iptv-backend/tests/test_url_normalize_ipv6.py`: `normalize_link("http://[::1]:8080/x/") == "http://[::1]:8080/x"`, `normalize_link("HTTP://[2001:DB8::1]/a/") == "http://[2001:db8::1]/a"`, `normalize_link("http://u:p@[::1]:80/") == "http://u:p@[::1]:80"`, `normalize_link("HTTP://Ex.com:8080/a/#f") == "http://ex.com:8080/a"`: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_url_normalize_ipv6.py -q` and the three IPv6 assertions fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Mixed naive and timezone-aware `created_at` values crash favorites ordering - `_created_at_key` in `iptv-backend/app/services/favorites_ordering.py` returns the raw value (or naive `datetime.min`), so `order_favorites` on one naive and one aware timestamp (or an aware one plus a missing one) raises `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` (probe: both cases). Fix: have `_created_at_key` return an aware UTC datetime: naive -> `replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)`, aware -> `astimezone(timezone.utc)`, missing -> `datetime.min.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)`. VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_favorites_ordering.py`: `order_favorites([{"name":"old","created_at":datetime(2020,1,1)},{"name":"new","created_at":datetime(2021,1,1,tzinfo=timezone.utc)}])` names are `["new","old"]`; an aware `+05:00` value `datetime(2021,1,1,3,tzinfo=timezone(timedelta(hours=5)))` sorts OLDER than `datetime(2020,12,31,23,tzinfo=timezone.utc)`; an entry with no `created_at` sorts last: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_favorites_ordering.py -q` and the new cases fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] A history entry with `created_at=None` crashes the trim helpers - `identify_excess_ids`, `trim_history` and `get_oldest_first` in `iptv-backend/app/services/history_trim.py` sort on the raw `created_at`, so one None raises `TypeError: '<' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'datetime.datetime'` (probe). Fix: sort with a shared key `_sort_key(entry)` returning `(created_at is not None, created_at)` so a missing timestamp counts as oldest (trimmed first, listed first by `get_oldest_first`). VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_history_trim.py` with `d1 < d2`: `[e["id"] for e in trim_history([{"id":1,"created_at":None},{"id":2,"created_at":d1},{"id":3,"created_at":d2}], 2)] == [3, 2]`, `identify_excess_ids(same, 2) == [1]`, `[e["id"] for e in get_oldest_first(same)] == [1, 2, 3]`: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_history_trim.py -q` and the new case fails before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `pick_now_next` crashes when the reference time and the programme times differ in timezone-awareness - `iptv-backend/app/services/epg_now_next.py` compares `start <= at_time < stop` directly, so a naive `at_time` against the aware programme times (or the reverse) raises `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` (probe), which `bulk_pick_now_next` then propagates for the whole batch. Fix: add `_as_utc(dt)` (naive -> `replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)`, aware -> `astimezone(timezone.utc)`) and apply it to `at_time` once and to each `start`/`stop` before comparing; still return the ORIGINAL program objects. VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_epg_now_next.py`: aware programmes `00:00-06:00` and `06:00-07:00` UTC with naive `at_time=datetime(2020,1,1,5)` give `(first, second)`; naive programmes with an aware `at_time` give the same pair; `bulk_pick_now_next` with the mixed inputs returns the same tuple for the stream id: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_epg_now_next.py tests/test_epg_now_next_bulk.py -q` and the mixed cases fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] The validator rejects uppercase schemes and accepts hostless or space-containing URLs - `M3UValidator._is_valid_url` in `iptv-backend/app/services/m3u_validator.py` matches `url.startswith(prefix)` case-sensitively, so `HTTP://a.com/x` is reported as an invalid stream URL, while `http://`, `http:///x` and `http://a b.com/x` all pass (probe: False, True, True, True). Fix: compare the scheme case-insensitively (`url.lower().startswith(prefix)`), then require a non-empty host after the scheme (`url[len(prefix):].split('/')[0]` non-empty) and no whitespace in the URL (`any(c.isspace() for c in url)` -> False); keep the 2000-char cap. VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_m3u_validator_url_and_classify.py`: `_is_valid_url` is True for `HTTP://a.com/x` and `rtmp://h/live`, False for `http://`, `http:///x`, `http://a b.com/x`: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_m3u_validator_url_and_classify.py tests/test_m3u_validator.py -q` and the new False/uppercase cases fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Deduping a watchlist by latest timestamp crashes on mixed naive/aware datetimes - `dedupe_watchlist` in `iptv-backend/app/services/watchlist_dedupe.py` does `current_ts > best_ts` on the raw values, so two entries for the same `stream_id` where one `added_at` is naive and the other aware raise `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` (probe). Fix: add `_comparable(ts)` that maps a naive datetime to UTC-aware (`replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)`), leaves aware values and non-datetimes alone, and compare `_comparable(current_ts) > _comparable(best_ts)`; the returned entries stay the originals. VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_watchlist_dedupe.py`: `dedupe_watchlist([{"stream_id":1,"t":datetime(2020,1,1)},{"stream_id":1,"t":datetime(2021,1,1,tzinfo=timezone.utc)},{"stream_id":2,"t":None}], timestamp_key="t")` returns the aware entry for stream 1 first then the stream 2 entry; the reverse input order keeps the same winner: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_watchlist_dedupe.py -q` and the new case fails before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `get_backoff_delay` raises OverflowError for a large attempt count - in `iptv-backend/app/services/stream_resilience.py` `delay = min(max_delay, base_delay * (2 ** attempt))` multiplies a float by a huge int, and `get_backoff_delay(1100)` raises `OverflowError: int too large to convert to float` (probe), which would escape a retry loop that keeps counting. Fix: compute the exponent safely, e.g. `delay = max_delay if attempt >= 64 else min(max_delay, base_delay * (2 ** attempt))` (jitter multiplier unchanged; the negative-attempt ValueError stays). VERIFY: add to `iptv-backend/tests/test_stream_resilience.py`: `15.0 <= get_backoff_delay(2000, base_delay=1.0, max_delay=30.0) <= 45.0`, same bounds for `attempt=1100`, `get_backoff_delay(0, 1.0, 30.0)` stays within `[0.5, 1.5]`, `attempt=-1` still raises ValueError: `cd iptv-backend && pytest tests/test_stream_resilience.py -q` and the large-attempt cases fail before the change. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [x] [P3] [decomposed] Playback heartbeats for a newly opened stream are dropped if another stream reported within 15 seconds - `reportHeartbeat` in `iptv-web/src/stores/history.ts` keeps one global `lastHeartbeatAt`, so after `reportHeartbeat(1, 10)`, an immediate `reportHeartbeat(2, 5)` returns null without calling the API and the new stream's first resume position is lost (probe: API called once, expected twice). Fix: also remember `lastHeartbeatStreamId` and apply the debounce only when `streamId === lastHeartbeatStreamId` (a different stream always goes through; `force` and `reset()` behave as before, reset also clears the id). - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] The streams store keeps a stale `total` after delete and create, so the item count and page count are wrong until the next fetch - `deleteStream` and `createStream` in `iptv-web/src/stores/streams.ts` update `streams` but never `total` (probe: load 2 streams with total 2, delete one, `store.total` is still 2 and `totalPages`/`hasMore` use it). Fix: in `deleteStream` after the successful API call, when the id was present in `streams.value`, set `total.value = Math.max(0, total.value - 1)`; in `createStream` after the push set `total.value += 1`. ### FEATURE: Player keyboard shortcuts - acceptance: with the player focused, Space or K toggles play, the arrow keys and J/L seek on video on demand, Up and Down change the volume, M mutes, F toggles fullscreen and the greater-than and less-than keys change the playback speed; live streams never seek or change speed; focus in a text field is ignored by the player itself; no control bar, layout or text changes.

Create `iptv-web/src/utils/__tests__/playerHotkeys.test.ts` with `describe('playerHotkeyAction')` covering every key above on a VOD, the four seek keys returning null for live while Space, M, F and the volume keys still work for live, and unknown keys (`'a'`, `'Enter'`, `''`) returning null. - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Add the function that applies a hotkey action to a player - in `iptv-web/src/utils/playerHotkeys.ts` export `interface HotkeyPlayer { paused(): boolean; play(): unknown; pause(): unknown; muted(value?: boolean): boolean | void; volume(value?: number): number | void; currentTime(value?: number): number | void; duration(): number; isFullscreen(): boolean; requestFullscreen(): unknown; exitFullscreen(): unknown }` and `applyPlayerHotkey(player: HotkeyPlayer, action: PlayerHotkeyAction): void`: `togglePlay` calls `play()` when `paused()` else `pause()`; `seek` sets `currentTime(next)` where `next = (currentTime() as number) + action.seconds` is clamped to `0` and, when `duration()` is a finite number, to `duration()`; `toggleMute` calls `muted(!muted())`; `volume` sets `volume(v)` with `v = Math.min(1, Math.max(0, Math.round(((volume() as number) + action.delta) * 100) / 100))`; `toggleFullscreen` calls `exitFullscreen()` when `isFullscreen()` else `requestFullscreen()`. ### FEATURE: XMLTV parsing robustness - acceptance: blank titles, channel names and optional text fields in an XMLTV guide never produce empty strings, a programme that ends at or before its start is skipped with a reported reason instead of being stored, and a negative episode number is not turned into a season or episode label; the parser's other behaviour, limits and return shape stay the same.

Create `iptv-backend/tests/test_xmltv_parser_blank_fields.py` with `test_blank_title_falls_back_to_untitled` and `test_blank_display_name_falls_back_to_channel_id` using `parse_xmltv_bytes(b"<tv>...</tv>")` with one programme (`start="20240101100000 +0000" stop="20240101110000 +0000" channel="a"`) and one `<channel id="chan.1">`.
